No slug passed through the inner plane. Stellar wind and local magnetic deflection curved the salvos after launch, bringing them together beyond the cradle boundary where Thren's braid accelerated.

The first salvo broke a particle channel. The second struck a vessel-strand near its maneuvering root. Thren rotated the remaining forms around the witness core and returned fire, cutting Kes's lead cruiser from bow to magazine. The human ship vented propellant and stayed on vector long enough to transmit correction for the third salvo.

Four more human ships disappeared under loyalist fire.

The ledger available through captured human broadcasts reached 72.

Kes's final salvo missed Thren's core. It struck the two surviving vessel-strands at unequal times, ruining their negotiated rotation. Last Burning's Edict tumbled away from the cradle plane with weapons intact but unable to align them. Two reserve escorts followed, firing lasers into sensor surfaces and maneuvering nodes rather than the central witness housing.

A damaged loyal braid tried to tow Thren back into firing geometry. Divided Glass intercepted and cut the tow line. The loyal braid fired. Both Veyr groups left the engagement disabled.

One human escort crossed too close to their collision and lost its radiator.

Human losses reached 73 ships.

Thren's witness core continued transmitting from inside the tumbling braid. RESTORE CONCORDANCE. CRADLE THREAT PERSISTS. ALL LOSS REMAINS PREFERABLE TO THIRD STERILIZATION.

Ilyr received more than language. The core carried constituent consent under severe damage. Thren's lineages had not changed their conclusion. They accepted civil destruction, human extermination, and their own death inside the same obligation.

Anchor Seven's shell weakened when Thren lost alignment. Ilyr tore the embedded strand free, leaving a length of living ceramic fused inside the control loop. The open phase propagated through every local sensor.

Human cruisers fired on the anchor from three permitted vectors. Open Current struck its power feeds. Patient Ember transmitted cradle-state verification during every impact. The structure cracked along its outer ring, vented charge into Mazarine's magnetic wind, and ceased supplying prediction.

The final anchor went dark.

Without concordance, Thren's damaged braid could not coordinate its vessel-strands. The witness core overheated as it tried to force agreement through severed paths. Ilyr sent an offer of custody: shut down weapons, release constituent records, accept continuity under external witness.

Thren refused.

A final particle discharge left the tumbling braid and missed Kes's crippled cruiser. Recoil drove the fractured vessel-strands against each other. Ceramic shells split around the central core. Thermal color climbed beyond any pattern that could preserve negotiated personhood.

The cessation record translated the next event into human tense with brutal simplicity: Thren dies with its braid.

No triumph entered Ilyr's surviving patterns. Thren had carried fear as law until law consumed every alternative. Its death removed command, not the history that had made command persuasive.

Battle continued in pockets. Some loyal braids fired after the final anchor failed. Human ships answered. Open Current and Divided Glass moved between them, taking damage from both sides. Patient Ember repeated the cradle telemetry: no destabilization, no high-power human channel, young patterns coherent.

Ilyr assembled a fleetwide cessation order without claiming authority over all Veyr.

ANCHORS DISABLED. THREN CONTINUITY ENDED. CRADLE SURVIVES. HUMAN HIGH-POWER PATH REMAINS UNUSED. ALL GROUPS CEASE FIRE LOCALLY, PRESERVE WITNESS CORES, AND RECOVER STRANDED CREWS. VERIFY THESE FACTS BEFORE OBEDIENCE.

The wording mattered. Ilyr asked each Reef to test the conditions rather than accept another prediction.

Three dissenting Reefs obeyed first. Unaffiliated braids followed after confirming Mazarine's field. Two groups loyal to Thren withdrew without acknowledgment. Others stopped because their magazines had emptied or their constituent patterns no longer supplied consent. Enough ceased that continued firing lost tactical purpose.

Obedience spread without a single boundary. One loyal group demanded human weapons cold before lowering its particle channels. A regional human cruiser made the reciprocal demand in the same interval. Open Current placed a damaged braid between them and transmitted both charge readings until each side could verify the other had reduced power. Elsewhere, a human escort stopped firing while its Veyr opponent continued for two more bursts; the escort rolled behind wreckage rather than answer. Patient Ember then cut the Veyr braid's targeting feed and repeated the local conditions. That exchange ended without anyone granting trust.

The staggered pattern protected the order from one false signal. Groups did not cease because Ilyr's name carried new supremacy. They ceased after neighboring behavior supplied evidence, then passed that evidence onward. Where facts differed, weapons remained ready. Where the cradle state and withdrawal vectors agreed, firing stopped.

Kes's reserve sent the six-second signature. Human regional groups began to hold fire at different moments, each after local verification. The last exchange ended near the stranded strike ships when a Veyr braid broke its firing track to push a human wreck away from a cradle streamer.

Witness reports converged slowly. The Veyr count settled at 84 Veyr braids destroyed or disabled. Some cores remained recoverable. Some persons would return altered. Human losses held at 73 ships, with rescue status unresolved across dozens of wrecks.

Within Mazarine, the unbound choruses continued their thermal movements. No witness language could promise what they might later become. Survival preserved that uncertainty.

Ilyr turned Consequence of Shelter's two functioning strands toward the nearest human strike craft. Open Current approached from the opposite side, weapons cold. Between them, they extended tow lines across the place where Anchor Seven had carried certainty.

Chapter 29 — The Armistice of Ash and Light

Meridian — Mara

Six months after Mazarine, two fleets waited outside Meridian's gate cones with weapons cold and targeting systems awake.

Mara Venn could see both formations through the convocation chamber's glass. Human cruisers held the prograde safety arc. Veyr braids occupied the anti-sunward plane in groups of three, each witness core transmitting identity but no fire-control geometry. Forty thousand kilometers separated the nearest hulls. The armistice text defined that distance as restraint. Every commander outside understood it as firing time.

Inside, the agreement lay across a table built from emergency bulkhead panels. Human delegates occupied one curve. Veyr witness projections occupied another, translated into thermal colors and geometric marks before appearing as speech. No one had arranged the room to suggest friendship.

Convener Olvara Pike turned the final page. "Clause Twelve remains disputed."

"Its inspection authority reaches military prediction hardware but not lineage archives," Mara said. "The Veyr want the same boundary around witness cores that we want around civilian medical networks."

A projection from Patient Ember altered from amber to white. "Human sunspindle control code can conceal a nursery attack inside civil infrastructure. Inspection limited by declared category cannot verify restraint."

"Then inspect behavior and hardware paths," Mara replied. "Not private memory. A joint team can confirm maximum field output, restoration kits, and physical isolation without reading every system attached to the spindle."

Pike studied her. "That language also protects the Commonwealth's sealed targeting doctrine."

"Reciprocity often resembles inconvenience."

The chamber had spent nineteen days building inconvenience into machinery. Joint inspection teams would include human engineers, Veyr thermal specialists, and reciprocal custody witnesses. Every operational sunspindle near the old Line would receive measured output limits. Every Veyr anchor rebuilt outside Mazarine would publish its coordination range and accept local monitors. Neither side could refuse access by relabeling military infrastructure as civilian or sacred. Neither side could demand unrestricted access to memory, communications, or biology.

A second clause established the cradle exclusion zone. Human warships and stellar-control packages would remain outside a surveyed boundary around Mazarine and eight other protected nurseries. Veyr combat braids would remain outside matched boundaries around Commonwealth sunspindle systems unless invited by a joint emergency board. Survey beacons belonged to neither fleet. Tampering triggered automatic suspension of gate passage and mandatory witness review, not immediate retaliatory fire.

Nothing in those terms asked trust to carry more than it could.

Enforcement relied on delays both sides could survive. An inspection dispute froze only the questioned route for seventy-two hours while every other corridor remained open. A verified undeclared weapons path triggered removal of that component, public release of the evidence, and a new vote by regional authorities. Rescue craft retained passage even during suspension. Neither civilization could turn one customs argument into permission for general attack.

The military clauses imposed costs before any breach. Human carriers pulled back from three gate cones they had occupied since the battle. Veyr groups dismantled temporary prediction relays along the same approaches. Commanders hated the symmetry for opposite reasons. Human officers argued that retreat surrendered hard-won warning time. Veyr witnesses argued that local coordination limits exposed Mazarine to another concealed strike. The agreement made both dangers visible and assigned witnesses to watch them.

Mara had rejected one proposed guarantee: automatic retaliation by preselected fleets. Its authors claimed certainty would deter violation. She had read too many delayed reports and corrupted firing pictures. The final language required local containment first, verified publication second, and civilian authorization before force expanded beyond the site of breach. No mechanism could prevent deliberate treachery. This one could prevent frightened procedure from manufacturing treachery faster than facts arrived.

Mara had argued hardest over restoration corridors. The Veyr first proposed routes only to the three isolated Burning Line systems, where replacement gate seeds and sunspindle tools could shorten reconstruction by years. Human delegates demanded Talassar and Ceral as well. Veyr delegates answered that open routes through former battle space exposed their nursery approaches. The compromise created timed corridors with published cargo, escort ceilings, rescue priority, and inspection at both ends.

"Clause Twelve can use Admiral Venn's hardware-path language," Pike said. "Do the Veyr witnesses concur?"

Nine projections did not answer together. Open Current accepted. Divided Glass requested a narrower definition of attached memory. Two witnesses withheld pending translation. Ilyr's damaged signature carried an objection to the phrase maximum safe output, since safety belonged to context rather than a permanent number.

Mara revised the wording on her pad. "Verified output envelope under declared configuration, with any configuration change reported before activation."

Ilyr's projection shifted to assent.

Pike sent the revision around the table. "Then we have a binding text."

No cheer followed. The human fleet outside still tracked Veyr movement. Veyr braids still mapped every charged weapon. At Talassar, survivors wanted reparations no inspection team could calculate. In several Reefs, Thren's supporters called the agreement a reward for invasion. Commonwealth delegates from Ceral called it a pardon for 2.7 million dead.

The armistice did not settle those claims. It stopped them from selecting firing solutions while courts, councils, and witness assemblies argued.

Each delegation applied a custody mark. Pike signed for the Commonwealth civil authority. Nine Veyr witnesses recorded distinct constituent consent, including objections that remained attached to their assent. Regional fleet commanders acknowledged the operational clauses separately. Any commander could suspend a local passage after verified breach, but no commander could expand that suspension into general attack without renewed civil authorization.

Mara signed only as outgoing emergency commander.

A clerk placed the Meridian command seal beside her hand. Its black surface carried scratches from three command vaults. She had used it to coordinate 312 ships, to accept operational control of Sable, to distribute refusal authority, and to send 191 combatants beyond the Burning Line. The object had no mechanism for grief. It authenticated whatever lawful hand held it.

"Emergency authority terminates on implementation," Pike said. "You may transfer it to the standing Defense Council."

Mara looked through the glass at formations holding their separate arcs. Her old instinct assembled them into one picture and searched for the order that would keep every interval exact. The picture remained useful. Ownership of every correction did not.

She pressed her palm to the seal one final time. The chamber verified her identity and opened the transfer record.

EMERGENCY LINE COMMAND RELINQUISHED. BOUNDED REGIONAL AUTHORITIES REVERT TO TREATY LAW. SABLE CUSTODY TRANSFERS TO JOINT DISMANTLEMENT.

Mara relinquished the seal to Pike.

"Your refusal inquiry remains open," the convener said quietly.

"It should."

"Some delegates want it dismissed as a successful decision."

"Success does not create authority after the fact."

Pike closed her hand around the seal. "You have become inconvenient."

"Reciprocity resembles that."

This time Pike almost smiled.

The chamber doors opened to legal officers, engineers, casualty representatives, and the first joint inspection roster. Mara stepped away from the central chair before anyone could direct her toward it again. Outside the glass, one human cruiser reduced target illumination. A Veyr braid answered by closing a particle channel. The distances did not change, but two firing clocks stopped counting.

Talassar — Tovan

Tovan Rhee found his mother's medical elevator stalled between Shelter Levels Four and Five.

"I left it broken so you would feel needed," Anja said from the access ladder.

"You left it broken because someone installed a flood-pump relay in the brake controller."

"The flood pump did not need it anymore."

"That explanation will comfort the patients hanging between floors."

Anja passed him a driver with the wrong head. Six months had added more gray to her hair and a pale scar along one forearm. The shelter around them still carried ring-fall damage: cracked wall tiles, patched pressure doors, and power lines clipped openly along the ceiling. Yet the status board showed no red medical transports waiting. Tovan accepted that as improvement.

Above the shelter, Talassar's western sea reflected a broken orbital ring. Most of the old structure had fallen or been pushed into safe decay paths after the attack. Surviving arcs crossed the sky at dusk like incomplete lines of metal. The first reconstruction girders now moved in lower orbit under civilian tugs, too small to see without magnification.

Tovan replaced the stolen relay with a contact block from his tool case. "Try it."

Anja keyed the maintenance panel. The elevator descended half a meter, complained through its guide rollers, then settled at Level Five. When the doors opened, three nurses and a patient in a mobility frame stared at Tovan.

"Fleet engineering," he said. "We offer delayed service and no warranty."

One nurse moved the patient out. "We filed this repair request four months ago."

"Then service has improved. Fleet usually needs a war."

Anja waited until the car emptied. "You came home with worse jokes."

"I came home."

They climbed to the surface through the repaired car. Salt air entered when the upper doors opened, mixed with the hot-metal smell of reconstruction yards. Talassar's shelf city had recovered power in blocks rather than districts. Tide-storage towers fed hospitals and water plants. Entire residential terraces remained dark where ring fragments had cut distribution trunks.

At the harbor, a wall carried the names of 113,000 dead or missing from the evacuation and strike. Search revisions continued to change the balance between those categories. Tovan found former deck workers among the names, then people Anja had known through the medical lift. He did not look for a lesson in the list.

His new assignment waited offshore in a temporary control building. Civil engineers, former carrier crews, and habitat constructors crowded around a model of the replacement orbital ring. The design used separated segments rather than one continuous structure. Any future cascade would encounter gaps wide enough to prevent the kind of lethal chain that had crossed Ceral's Glass Orbit and Talassar's sky.

"We lost efficiency," the project director told him. "Four percent more station-keeping fuel. Nine percent more transfer delay between segments."

"We gained places for debris to stop."

"The treasury prefers efficiency."

"Debris has no treasury."

Tovan opened the thermal plan. New girders would arrive through a restoration corridor under the armistice. Veyr ceramic fabricators could produce field-resistant joints, but Talassar's assembly unions had refused unmonitored components. The joint inspection team therefore tested every batch in a human yard while Veyr witnesses observed the test and retained samples. No one called the process elegant. It produced joints both sides could verify.

The first ring segment required three coolant manifolds adapted from carrier practice. Tovan had spent the morning discovering that planetary construction crews used the word temporary with the same dishonesty as Fleet. Hoses ran through permanent wall cuts. Prototype pumps had already acquired maintenance schedules. A rack labeled FOR TEST ONLY carried the city's live orbital traffic model.

He felt at home despite himself.

A display showed the nine Mazarine strike craft in salvage orbit. Every hull had been written off after heat-spine delamination. Reciprocal rescue had recovered all nine crews before life support failed, though burns and pressure injuries kept several people in treatment. Lantern Nine's commercial capacitor banks survived only as fused metal held in evidence containers. The armistice inspectors had examined those remains first because restraint needed a material record.

"Your package design goes into the joint archive tomorrow," the director said. "Both versions."

Tovan knew what she meant. High-power cradle-collapse paths and low-power anchor pulses would remain under reciprocal custody. Deleting the knowledge could not delete the physics. Hiding it would repeat Thren's censored margins in another language.

"Archive the failed models too," he said. "Especially the compensation branches."

"They nearly climbed into the dangerous envelope."

"Which gives inspectors something to compare against the next clever improvement."

Outside, a tug lifted the first segmented-ring joint from a barge. Its drive pushed water flat across the harbor. The joint rose on cables, black composite dripping seawater, while yard crews called clearances in three languages.

Anja joined Tovan at the control-building door. "Does this mean you are staying?"

"The ring plan has fourteen years of work and six months of funding. Someone should improve the ratio."

"Your old carrier?"

"Common Measure will never fight again. They are towing her to Meridian as a rescue school and thermal test hull. Half the deck crew already transferred here."

Anja watched the joint climb. "I refused a berth because this world needed the lift. You stayed with the fleet because the fleet might reach this world. We both made ourselves essential in places with replacement staff."

"The elevator had no replacement relay."

"You see my point."

Tovan did. He disliked it on principle.

A yard alarm marked the joint reaching transfer altitude. New orbital tugs took the load and began the slow lift toward the first segment. Their paths appeared on the harbor display as separate curves with broad safety gaps.

"I will stay through commissioning," he said. "After that, I will become difficult to retain."

"Good. Your room is full of pump parts."

"Then I will sleep at the yard."

Anja handed him the wrong driver again. He put it in his pocket. Above them, the first black joint crossed the surviving arc without touching it, leaving an intentional gap against the blue Talassar sky.

Mazarine — Ilyr

Ilyr-of-Nine-Reefs entered the witness geometry as one damaged person among nine.

Consequence of Shelter retained two functioning vessel-strands and a witness core whose records ended abruptly where two lineage patterns had burned away. Repairs could rebuild ceramic hull and restore maneuvering symmetry. They could not recreate the lost negotiation. Ilyr's seven surviving patterns had learned to leave space in every consent record for voices that would not return.

Around Mazarine, the former battle plane carried survey beacons instead of concordance anchors. Open Current maintained three. A human inspection tender maintained three more. The remaining markers alternated custody every twelve hours. Their signals described position and cradle field conditions without supplying firing prediction.

The armistice appointed nine Veyr witnesses from nine political constituencies. Ilyr represented neither all lineages nor all dissent. Patient Ember sent a cautious strategist who had opposed firing on Thren but distrusted human stellar engineering. Divided Glass sent a custody jurist. Two witnesses came from Reefs that had remained loyal until Anchor Seven failed. One had lost a constituent vessel to Kes's reserve. Their agreement began with different injuries.

Humanity appointed reciprocal witnesses as well: engineers, rescue-law officers, a Talassan survivor, and two commanders whose ships had fought at Mazarine. None possessed unilateral inspection authority. Any verified report required marks from both civilizations and publication of attached objections.

Ilyr preferred the friction. A single savior could become another anchor.

The first repair convoy waited outside the old Burning Line route marker. Human charts still colored the gate chain amber-red, though the physical corridor contained no wall. Twelve cargo tugs carried gate-seed assemblies, sunspindle repair frames, medical stores, and habitat pressure shells. Their declared destinations included Orison's Reach, Caer Sola, and Ninth Meridian. Later restoration corridors would carry girders to Talassar and shielding to Ceral.

Three Veyr braids approached as escort. Their particle channels stayed physically open for inspection and electrically cold. Human convoy weapons remained sealed under witness tags. Both groups tracked each other anyway.

A cargo manifest entered Ilyr's core. The human list included stellar field controllers. Their mass and shielding matched ordinary sunspindle repair equipment, but the same category had once concealed the components used in Sable packages.

Patient Ember's witness requested a hold. "Declared use does not establish bounded output."

The Talassan human inspector answered through translation. "Agreed. Containers Four through Seven are available for hardware-path review. Memory modules remain sealed unless the physical inspection finds an undeclared branch."

No offense entered the exchange. No trust entered it either.

Joint teams opened the containers in vacuum. Human engineers exposed bus architecture and current limits. Veyr thermal specialists mapped every route from control module to field coupler. Ilyr compared the shapes against recovered nursery-key records. The repair controllers could restart ordinary magnetic loops. They lacked the synchronized overload channels, restoration bridges, and protected timing stores required for a cradle strike.

A second team inspected the gate seeds for hidden concordance hardware. None appeared. Objections attached to two ambiguous memory partitions, so those units remained behind under reciprocal seal. The convoy accepted the delay rather than demand passage as proof of goodwill.

Inspection consumed eleven hours.

During that time, Mazarine's unbound choruses moved through stable magnetic arches. Human instruments observed only field behavior permitted under the cradle exclusion zone. Veyr witnesses observed the human instruments. Survey beacons published both readings to civil archives on each side of the Line.

At the final custody review, Ilyr recorded the conclusion.

"Verified repair cargo. Verified medical and habitat cargo. Two control units withheld for secondary review. The admitted convoy carries no nursery keys."

All nine Veyr witnesses marked the record separately. The human panel added its marks. Patient Ember retained an objection about future software changes. The Talassan inspector retained an objection about indefinite seizure. Publication carried both.

Passage authority opened.

The twelve tugs accelerated toward the gate lens in a broad civilian formation. Veyr escorts matched their outer vectors rather than enclosing them. One escort came from a Reef that had fought beside Thren until the final anchor failed. Its witness core carried records of human coil fire and Veyr civil loss. It held formation anyway because the corridor terms supplied a duty narrower than forgiveness.

Ilyr guided Consequence of Shelter beside the lead repair tug. Through thermal imaging, the cargo hull showed patched radiator panels and a drive older than the war. Its registry traced back to Lantern Nine.

The tugmaster transmitted a plain human query. "Witness escort, our prograde drift reads high by three meters per second. Confirm your safe cone."

Ilyr measured the approach without concordance. "Safe cone confirmed if your next burn remains below declared limit. We will match locally."

"Understood. Try not to predict us."

Seven surviving patterns examined the humor and did not reach consensus.

The gate lens brightened ahead. Beyond it waited ruined infrastructure, isolated populations, and years of work that no agreement could abbreviate. The convoy did not carry absolution. It carried pressure shells, medical stores, field frames, and one replacement seed.

Human tugs crossed first under Veyr escort. Consequence of Shelter followed beside the final cargo hull. On the shared chart, the lead repair ship passed the amber-red route marker, and twelve verified drives entered the old Line together.
